    74	/**
    75	 * @param {HTMLElement} container
    76	 * @param {object[]} checks
    77	 * @param {(check: object) => void} onSelect
    78	 * @param {Map<string, string>} crops check id -> data URL
    79	 */
    80	export function renderChecklist(container, checks, onSelect, crops) {
    81	  const rows = checks.map((c) => {
    82	    const tr = el("tr", { dataset: { check: c.id }, tabindex: "-1" }, [
    83	      el("td", {}, statusTag(c.status)),
    84	      el("td", {}, [
    85	        el("div", { class: "check-label", text: c.label }),
    86	        el("div", { class: "check-note", text: c.note }),
    87	        c.rule ? el("div", { class: "rule", text: c.rule }) : null,
    88	      ]),
    89	      el("td", { class: "expected", text: c.expected || "—" }),
    90	      el("td", {}, [
    91	        el("div", { class: "found", text: c.found || (c.status === "not_found" ? "nothing similar found" : "—") }),
    92	        crops.get(c.id) ? el("img", { class: "crop", src: crops.get(c.id), alt: `Label region where "${c.found || ""}" was found` }) : null,
    93	      ]),
    94	    ]);
    95	    tr.addEventListener("click", () => onSelect(c));
    96	    tr.addEventListener("keydown", (e) => { if (e.key === "Enter" || e.key === " ") { e.preventDefault(); onSelect(c); } });
    97	    tr.tabIndex = 0;
    98	    return tr;
    99	  });
   100	  container.replaceChildren(
   101	    el("table", { class: "usa-table usa-table--stacked checklist width-full" }, [
   102	      el("thead", {}, el("tr", {}, [
   103	        el("th", { scope: "col", text: "Status" }), el("th", { scope: "col", text: "Check" }),
   104	        el("th", { scope: "col", text: "Application says" }), el("th", { scope: "col", text: "Label says" }),
   105	      ])),
   106	      el("tbody", {}, rows),
   107	    ]),
   108	  );
   109	}
   110	
   111	function renderDiff(diff) {
   112	  // "-a b | +c" -> spans
   113	  const wrap = el("span", { class: "diff" });
   114	  for (const part of diff.split(" | ")) {
   115	    if (part.startsWith("-")) wrap.append(el("span", { class: "del", text: part.slice(1) }), " ");
   116	    else if (part.startsWith("+")) wrap.append(el("span", { class: "ins", text: part.slice(1) }), " ");
   117	    else wrap.append(part, " ");
   118	  }
   119	  return wrap;
   120	}
   121	
   122	export function renderWarning(container, w, onSelect) {
   123	  const overall = !w.present ? "not_found" : w.assessment === "exact" && w.anchor_caps === "match" ? "match"
   124	    : w.assessment === "wording" ? "mismatch" : "needs_review";
   125	  const card = el("div", { class: "warning-card" }, [
   126	    el("div", { class: "warning-head" }, [
   127	      statusTag(overall),
   128	      el("span", { class: "text-bold", text: !w.present ? "No warning statement found" : w.exact ? "Wording is exact" : "Wording is not exact" }),
   129	      w.present ? el("button", { type: "button", class: "usa-button usa-button--unstyled", text: "Show on label", onclick: () => onSelect({ id: "warning", evidence: w.evidence }) }) : null,
   130	    ]),
   131	    w.present ? el("dl", { class: "margin-top-2" }, [
   132	      el("dt", { class: "text-bold", text: "As read from the label" }),
   133	      el("dd", { class: "margin-left-0 margin-top-05" }, el("div", { class: "found-text", text: w.found_text })),
   134	      w.diff ? el("dt", { class: "text-bold margin-top-2", text: "Differences from the required text" }) : null,
   135	      w.diff ? el("dd", { class: "margin-left-0 margin-top-05" }, renderDiff(w.diff)) : null,
   136	      el("dt", { class: "text-bold margin-top-2", text: "Format checks" }),
   137	      el("dd", { class: "margin-left-0 margin-top-05" }, el("ul", { class: "usa-list usa-list--unstyled" }, [
   138	        el("li", {}, [statusTag(w.anchor_caps), " ", "GOVERNMENT WARNING in capital letters"]),
   139	        el("li", { class: "margin-top-05" }, [statusTag(w.anchor_bold), " ", "GOVERNMENT WARNING in bold type"]),
   140	        el("li", { class: "margin-top-05" }, [statusTag(w.body_not_bold), " ", "Remainder of the statement not in bold"]),
   141	      ])),
   142	    ]) : null,
   143	    el("ul", { class: "usa-list" }, w.notes.map((n) => el("li", { text: n }))),
   144	    el("p", { class: "rule text-base", text: w.rule }),
   145	  ]);
   146	  container.replaceChildren(card);
   147	}
   148	
   149	/**
   150	 * Render each image with an SVG overlay in canonical coordinates.
   151	 * @returns {Map<number, SVGSVGElement>} image index -> overlay
   152	 */
   153	export function renderFigures(container, images, files, lines) {
   154	  container.replaceChildren();
   155	  const overlays = new Map();
   156	  images.forEach((im, i) => {
   157	    const url = URL.createObjectURL(files[i]);
   158	    const img = el("img", { src: url, alt: `Label image ${i + 1}${im.filename ? `: ${im.filename}` : ""}` });
   159	    img.addEventListener("load", () => URL.revokeObjectURL(url), { once: true });
   160	    const svg = document.createElementNS("http://www.w3.org/2000/svg", "svg");
   161	    svg.setAttribute("class", "overlay");
   162	    svg.setAttribute("viewBox", `0 0 ${im.width} ${im.height}`);
   163	    svg.setAttribute("preserveAspectRatio", "none");
   164	    svg.setAttribute("aria-hidden", "true");
   165	    overlays.set(i, svg);
   166	    const readLines = lines.filter((l) => l.image_index === i).length;
   167	    const q = im.quality;
   168	    const caption = [
   169	      im.filename || `Image ${i + 1}`, `${im.width} × ${im.height}`, `${readLines} line${readLines === 1 ? "" : "s"} read`,
   170	      im.rotated_degrees ? `rotated ${im.rotated_degrees}° to read` : null,
   171	      q.readable ? null : `Not readable: ${q.reason || ""}`,
   172	    ].filter(Boolean).join(" · ");
   173	    container.append(el("figure", { class: "label-figure" }, [
   174	      el("div", { class: "label-stage" }, [img, svg]),
   175	      el("figcaption", { text: caption }),
   176	    ]));
   177	  });
   178	  return overlays;
   179	}
   180	
   181	function polygonFor(quad, cls) {
   182	  const p = document.createElementNS("http://www.w3.org/2000/svg", "polygon");
   183	  p.setAttribute("points", quad.map(([x, y]) => `${x},${y}`).join(" "));
   184	  if (cls) p.setAttribute("class", cls);
   185	  return p;
   186	}
   187	
   188	/** Draw all evidence faintly; the active check's evidence strongly. */
   189	export function drawOverlays(overlays, checks, warning, activeId) {
   190	  for (const svg of overlays.values()) svg.replaceChildren();
   191	  const items = [...checks.map((c) => ({ id: c.id, status: c.status, evidence: c.evidence })),
   192	    { id: "warning", status: warning.exact ? "match" : "needs_review", evidence: warning.evidence || [] }];
   193	  for (const it of items) {
   194	    for (const ev of it.evidence) {
   195	      const svg = overlays.get(ev.image_index);
   196	      if (!svg) continue;
   197	      const bad = it.status === "mismatch" || it.status === "not_found";
   198	      svg.append(polygonFor(ev.box, [it.id === activeId ? "is-active" : "", bad ? "is-bad" : ""].join(" ").trim()));
   199	    }
   200	  }
   201	}
   202	
   203	/**
   204	 * Cut evidence crops from the original images. Uses the browser's EXIF-aware decoder so the
   205	 * bitmap is in the same oriented space as the server's coordinates; verifies dimensions match.
   206	 * @returns {Promise<Map<string, string>>} check id -> data URL
   207	 */
   208	export async function makeCrops(files, images, checks) {
   209	  const crops = new Map();
   210	  const bitmaps = new Map();
   211	  for (const c of checks) {
   212	    const ev = c.evidence && c.evidence[0];
   213	    if (!ev) continue;
   214	    const im = images[ev.image_index];
   215	    const file = files[ev.image_index];
   216	    if (!im || !file) continue;
   217	    try {
   218	      if (!bitmaps.has(ev.image_index)) {
   219	        bitmaps.set(ev.image_index, await createImageBitmap(file, { imageOrientation: "from-image" }));
   220	      }
   221	      const bmp = bitmaps.get(ev.image_index);
   222	      if (Math.abs(bmp.width - im.width) > 2 || Math.abs(bmp.height - im.height) > 2) {
   223	        continue; // browser and server disagree on orientation for this file: no crop rather than a wrong one
   224	      }
   225	      const sx = 1, sy = 1;
   226	      const xs = c.evidence.flatMap((e) => e.box.map((p) => p[0]));
   227	      const ys = c.evidence.flatMap((e) => e.box.map((p) => p[1]));
   228	      const pad = 8;
   229	      const x0 = Math.max(0, Math.min(...xs) - pad), y0 = Math.max(0, Math.min(...ys) - pad);
   230	      const x1 = Math.min(im.width, Math.max(...xs) + pad), y1 = Math.min(im.height, Math.max(...ys) + pad);
   231	      const w = Math.max(1, x1 - x0), h = Math.max(1, y1 - y0);
   232	      const scale = Math.min(1, 640 / w, 120 / h);
   233	      const canvas = document.createElement("canvas");
   234	      canvas.width = Math.round(w * scale); canvas.height = Math.round(h * scale);
   235	      canvas.getContext("2d").drawImage(bmp, x0 * sx, y0 * sy, w * sx, h * sy, 0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
   236	      crops.set(c.id, canvas.toDataURL("image/png"));
   237	    } catch { /* crops are a convenience; never fail the result over them */ }
   238	  }
   239	  for (const b of bitmaps.values()) b.close?.();
   240	  return crops;
   241	}
   242	
   243	export function renderOcrLines(container, lines) {
   244	  const byImage = new Map();
   245	  for (const l of lines) (byImage.get(l.image_index) || byImage.set(l.image_index, []).get(l.image_index)).push(l);
   246	  container.replaceChildren(...[...byImage.entries()].map(([idx, ls]) =>
   247	    el("div", {}, [
   248	      el("h4", { class: "margin-bottom-05", text: `Image ${idx + 1}` }),
   249	      el("ol", { class: "ocr-lines" }, ls.map((l) => el("li", { text: `${l.text}  (${Math.round(l.confidence * 100)}%)` }))),
   250	    ])));
   251	}

===== FILE: docs/APPROACH.md =====
     1	# Approach, tools, assumptions
     2	
     3	Two pages, as the brief asked. Depth lives in the linked documents.
     4	
     5	## What I built and why
     6	
     7	A compliance agent uploads the label images for one application, enters what the application
     8	says, and gets a checklist in a few seconds: brand, class/type, alcohol content, net contents,
     9	bottler, country of origin, and the government warning checked word for word. Every finding is
    10	tied to the exact pixels it came from, so the agent looks at the evidence instead of hunting on
    11	the label. A batch screen does the same for a folder of images and a spreadsheet of applications,
    12	streaming results, recording decisions, and exporting them. **The tool recommends; the agent
    13	decides.** Nothing is stored.
    14	
    15	The brief's interviews carried the real requirements, and the authors bolded four of them: about
    16	five seconds, usable by anyone, batch uploads, and an exact warning. The architecture follows
    17	from those four plus Marcus's firewall.
    18	
    19	- **Five seconds** ruled out a cloud vision API on a government network with blocked egress and
    20	  also, honestly, ruled it out on latency. OCR runs in-process on a small neural text detector and
    21	  recognizer (PP-OCR via ONNX Runtime), chosen by measurement over Tesseract and over larger
    22	  models (`docs/BAKEOFF.md`, `docs/OCR_EVAL.md`). The two images of an application are read in
    23	  parallel. Timing is printed on every result.
    24	- **Usable by anyone** meant one screen, two numbered steps, one button, three one-click samples,
    25	  and the U.S. Web Design System, which is what federal agents already see every day and is built
    26	  for Section 508. Statuses are always an icon, a word and a color.
    27	- **Batch** meant the browser orchestrates: it reads each image once, pairs images to applications
    28	  by an explicit rule (a CSV column or a filename prefix; leftovers are assigned by hand, never
    29	  guessed), compares each application as soon as its images are read, and streams rows in. The
    30	  bulk compare endpoint serves scripted clients. The server stays stateless, which is also why it
    31	  scales by adding replicas and why a refresh clears the session (the page says so).
    32	- **Exact warning** meant a character-level comparison with the regulation text pulled from the
    33	  eCFR API, where only an exact match passes. The interesting engineering is separating OCR noise
    34	  (a dropped colon, a `0` read as `O`) from a wording change (`can` for `may`). Noise is "Needs
    35	  review" with a diff; a wording change is a mismatch.
    36	
    37	Two decisions I would defend first. Automatic approval was never on the table, because Dave is
    38	right that judgment is the job and because federal AI guidance requires human oversight for
    39	decisions like this. And no LLM sits in the verification path: the firewall forbids it, the
    40	five-second budget punishes it, and a compliance tool should give the same answer for the same
    41	label every time, with every finding traceable to pixels. The engine sits behind a small
    42	interface (`app/ocr/base.py`); a second-opinion provider would implement it and be pointed at an
    43	approved endpoint inside the agency boundary. None is wired in.
    44	
    45	## How it works
    46	
    47	`images.py` sniffs the file type by signature, guards against oversized images, applies EXIF
    48	orientation, downscales for OCR and keeps the transform so every box can be mapped back to the
    49	original image. `ocr/` holds the engine and a worker pool with one engine per thread; admission
    50	control gives interactive requests priority and refuses batch traffic with a 429 and Retry-After
    51	rather than queueing it. `pipeline/` is pure functions with no I/O: normalization, parsers for
    52	alcohol and net contents (the same parser runs on the application value and the label text, so
    53	"45% Alc./Vol. (90 Proof)" compares as numbers), fuzzy matching with three outcomes, the warning
    54	comparator, standards of fill, and the verdict. `services.py` orchestrates; `routes/` is thin.
    55	The frontend is vanilla JavaScript modules with no build step, so a reviewer can read all of it.
    56	
    57	## Tools used
    58	
    59	- Python 3.12, FastAPI, Pydantic, NumPy, Pillow, RapidFuzz; RapidOCR with PP-OCR models on ONNX
    60	  Runtime (vendored, hash-pinned, no downloads); U.S. Web Design System 3.14 (vendored).
    61	- pytest, ruff, mypy in strict mode; Playwright for headless browser checks; Docker; Azure
    62	  Container Apps.
    63	- **AI coding agents, openly:** Claude Code as the builder and Codex as an independent reviewer,
    64	  directed by me. The requirements analysis, the architecture, the review passes, and every
    65	  decision in `docs/DECISIONS.md` are mine; where the builder's first proposal was wrong, the log
    66	  says so and says who caught it. The design review and its dispositions are in `docs/reviews/`.
    67	  Commits carry the agent trailer.
    68	
    69	## Assumptions (gaps filled without asking)
    70	
    71	1. The primary input is label artwork as submitted to COLA; photographs are a secondary case.
    72	2. An application has one or more label images (front, back, neck); the warning is usually on
    73	   the back, so images are merged before any check.
    74	3. Application values are entered as written, including formatting like "(90 Proof)".
    75	4. Case-only and accent-only differences between application and label are not defects.
    76	5. Bottler and origin are checked when the application supplies them; origin is required when the
    77	   application is marked imported.
    78	6. Wine may omit a numeric alcohol statement when designated table or light wine; malt beverages
    79	   may omit it under federal rules; both are reported, not flagged.
    80	7. The five-second expectation applies to one application through the interface, not to a batch.
    81	8. "Deployed URL we can access" means a public prototype without sign-in; the production path is
    82	   described, not faked.
    83	9. Reviewers may test from a managed government browser, so the page uses no CDN, no WebSockets,
    84	   and nothing newer than widely supported web platform features.
    85	
    86	## Trade-offs and limitations
    87	
    88	Bold type and physical type size are not assessed and are shown as "Not checked" rather than
    89	guessed. Photographs are best effort. Class/type validity, age statements and laboratory
    90	tolerances are out of scope. Fuzzy pairing of unlabeled images is deliberately absent. The full
    91	list, with the enforced limits and the measured numbers, is in `docs/LIMITS.md`; the regulatory
    92	basis of every check is in `docs/REGULATIONS.md`; security and data handling in `docs/SECURITY.md`.
    93	
    94	## Path to production
    95	
    96	Behind the agency identity provider (Entra ID with PIV); Azure Government; an audit record of
    97	decisions with a retention policy; model versions promoted only after the evaluation in
    98	`docs/EVAL.md` passes; horizontal scaling by replicas; COLA integration through the same JSON API.

===== FILE: docs/DECISIONS.md =====
     1	# Decision log
     2	
     3	Dated, in the order they were made. Each entry says what was decided, why, and what evidence or
     4	review changed it. Decisions marked **(corrected)** record a first proposal that was wrong and
     5	how it was caught. Owner of every decision: Daniel R. Cooley. Builder: Claude Code. Reviewer: Codex.
     6	
     7	| ID | Date | Decision | Why | Evidence / source |
     8	|---|---|---|---|---|
     9	| D-001 | 2026-09-03 | Python + FastAPI backend, vanilla HTML/JS frontend styled with the U.S. Web Design System, one container. | Primary language of the author; strongest OCR ecosystem; one URL; no build step; no CDN so it works behind a restrictive firewall; USWDS is the federal standard and Section 508 ready. | Brief: Marcus on firewall; Sarah on simplicity. |
    10	| D-002 | 2026-09-03 | OCR runs in-process. No LLM or cloud vision API in the core verification path. Pluggable provider interface for an optional, off-by-default second opinion. | The brief's firewall note and 5-second limit rule cloud calls out; determinism and pixel-traceable findings matter for a government audit tool. | Brief: Marcus, Sarah. Review 001 agreed. |
    11	| D-003 | 2026-09-03 | The tool recommends; the agent decides. No auto-approve or auto-reject anywhere. | Dave's "you need judgment"; federal AI guidance requires human oversight for rights-impacting decisions. | Brief: Dave. |
    12	| D-004 | 2026-09-03 | No Treasury or TTB seals. Prototype banner instead. Configurable agency branding slot left empty in the public demo. | Federal law restricts use of Treasury symbols in ways implying endorsement; the reviewers' own seal in the invitation email is a signature graphic, not a project asset. | Discussion with author. |
    13	| D-005 | 2026-09-03 | RapidOCR (ONNX Runtime) is the sole engine; Tesseract dropped. Final configuration: bundled PP-OCRv6 small detector and recognizer, angle classifier off, detector side 640, input 1280 px, recognizer batch 16, one intra-op thread per engine. Models vendored with SHA-256. | Measured on 46 synthetic images: RapidOCR recovered ABV 90-100% and net contents 90-100% across degradations; Tesseract was 3x faster but lost small text (ABV 76%, net 71%) and failed on sideways images. Among RapidOCR configurations, the bundled small models matched or beat the v4 and English models on accuracy at 40-50% less single-thread time, and they need no download at all. | docs/BAKEOFF.md, docs/OCR_EVAL.md (runs A-H). |
    14	| D-006 | 2026-09-03 | Preprocessing is minimal: EXIF transpose, downscale, 90-degree retry on low confidence. **(corrected)** | The plan had deskew, CLAHE, glare suppression and zoom passes. Measurement showed the engine already handles glare, blur, perspective, low contrast and small text; only sideways images failed. | docs/BAKEOFF.md; Review 001 3.2. |
    15	| D-007 | 2026-09-03 | Pin Python 3.12 in the container and CI; build the lock on linux/amd64; vendor model files with SHA-256; no downloads at build or run. **(corrected)** | On Python 3.13 pip silently resolved the OCR library to a 2023 release whose thread settings were ignored, which produced misleading concurrency numbers. | Review 001 4.2 and 4.3; perf run. |
    16	| D-008 | 2026-09-03 | A COLA application is a set of images (front, back, neck), merged before checks. **(corrected)** | First plan assumed one image per application; the warning is usually on the back label, so that design would flag every front label as missing the warning. | Author review pass 2. |
    17	| D-009 | 2026-09-03 | Primary input is label artwork; photographs are a best-effort secondary tier reported separately. **(corrected)** | Brief says agents look at "label artwork"; Jenny calls photos "maybe out of scope". | Author review pass 3. |
    18	| D-010 | 2026-09-03 | Application fields are accepted as strings exactly as written (e.g. "45% Alc./Vol. (90 Proof)"); the same parser runs on both sides; comparison is numeric. **(corrected)** | The brief's sample shows string fields; a bare numeric field would have failed the sample. | Author review pass 3. |
    19	| D-011 | 2026-09-03 | Warning check: exact match is the only Pass. Near matches are Needs review with a character diff. Anchor caps and bold, body not bold, contrast and size are Needs review findings with evidence. | 27 CFR 16.21 text and 16.22 format verified from the eCFR API; Jenny: "It has to be exact." Review 001 1.4 tightened an earlier "OCR-tolerant" design. | docs/REGULATIONS.md; Review 001. |
    20	| D-012 | 2026-09-03 | Heuristic findings never produce a failure; only clear text or numeric mismatches do. | Keeps the false-alarm rate low (Dave's phone-system story) and leaves judgment with the agent. | Author review pass 5. |
    21	| D-013 | 2026-09-03 | Bottler name/address and country of origin are core comparisons when supplied; origin is required when the application is marked imported. **(corrected)** | The brief lists both as required label elements; the plan had demoted them to optional. | Review 001 1.6, 6.7. |
    22	| D-014 | 2026-09-03 | Batch pairing defaults to explicit filenames or a CSV column; fuzzy pairing is a confirmable suggestion, never automatic. **(corrected)** | Back labels often carry no brand, ABV or net contents, so automatic pairing could attach them to the wrong application with confidence. | Review 001 2.2. |
    23	| D-015 | 2026-09-03 | One deployment host (Azure Container Apps) plus a tested local Docker command; a second host is an emergency plan, not a parallel deployment. **(corrected)** | Two hosts doubled operational surface for a one-week prototype; Azure domains also have better odds of being reachable from a government network. | Review 001 2.1. |
    24	| D-016 | 2026-09-03 | One priority-aware capacity limiter; cooperative pass budgets instead of pretend timeouts; streaming size caps; tmpfs for temporary files. **(corrected)** | Two semaphores could exceed CPU; a deadline cannot stop a running inference; multipart can spool to disk. | Review 001 2.4, 2.5, 2.8. |
    25	| D-017 | 2026-09-03 | Standards of fill checked against the application's stated net contents only, Needs review only. | Real agents check container sizes; using application data removes OCR risk; the lists changed in January 2025 so a hard failure would be wrong. | docs/REGULATIONS.md; Review 001 3.4 (narrowed rather than cut). |
    26	| D-018 | 2026-09-03 | Acceptance gate for speed: deployed, end-to-end, front+back application p95 under 5 seconds under concurrent batch load. | The authors bolded the 5-second sentence; a per-image number on a laptop is not the same claim. | Brief emphasis; Review 001 1.1. |
    27	| D-019 | 2026-09-03 | AI assistance is disclosed openly: commit trailers kept, Claude Code listed under tools used, this log records where the AI's first proposal was wrong. | The role is AI-directed engineering and the panel knows the workflow; the direction and correction are the work product. | Author. |
    28	| D-020 | 2026-09-03 | OCR workers are a thread pool, one engine per thread with one ONNX intra-op thread each, N = vCPUs. Process pool kept only as a crash-isolation option. **(corrected twice)** | First perf run showed no scaling, which turned out to be the old library ignoring thread settings. With real controls, 2 threads gave 1.84x and 4 gave 3.1x the single-worker throughput, and a process pool gave identical numbers (0.79 vs 0.79 img/s at 2 workers; 1.33 vs 1.31 at 4). Threads are simpler and share memory. | docs/OCR_EVAL.md runs D, T, P. |
    29	| D-021 | 2026-09-03 | Per-image single-core cost is about 2.4 s on a laptop core, so the 5-second application gate needs at least 2 real vCPUs and the two images of an application must run in parallel. Hosting is chosen for dedicated CPU and measured cost, not theme. | Measured (run D). First estimate of Azure Container Apps at ~$150/month assumed a fully busy container; the idle rate that applies most of a review window puts a 2 vCPU / 4 GiB always-on app nearer $45-60/month, inside the $200 first-month credit. Dedicated 2-vCPU machines on Fly or Hetzner cost $8-62/month. **(corrected)** cost estimate. | docs/OCR_EVAL.md; provider price lists (to be confirmed at signup). |
    30	| D-022 | 2026-09-03 | The warning report carries an explicit `assessment` (exact, case, noise, wording, absent) and the verdict keys off it: exact passes; case and noise are Needs review; wording and absent are issues. Similarity thresholds no longer decide. | Thresholds blurred the one distinction that matters to Jenny: OCR noise versus a changed word. Word-level classification is explainable in one sentence. | Evaluation run 1 on the synthetic corpus; `tests/unit/test_warning.py`. |
    31	| D-023 | 2026-09-03 | Accents in the OCR read of the warning are removed before the literal comparison; a single-character difference inside a word of five or more letters, and digit/letter confusables including `1`/`i`, count as noise. | The recognizer (a multilingual model) emits "alcoholič" and "drivé" for plain letters; the regulation text has no accented letters, so an accent is recognition noise by definition. Real wording changes replace whole words. **(corrected)** after evaluation run 1 showed clean artwork failing the warning check. | `docs/EVAL.md` history; `tests/unit/test_warning.py`. |
    32	| D-024 | 2026-09-03 | Reading order groups lines into rows by vertical overlap (at least half the shorter line's height), then sorts left to right. | Height-bucket sorting scrambled labels with mixed text sizes or tight line spacing, which truncated the warning span. **(corrected twice)**: first version divided by each line's own height, second by fixed buckets. | `tests/unit/test_match.py::test_reading_order_uses_vertical_overlap_not_height_buckets`. |
    33	| D-025 | 2026-09-03 | Batch-mode requests (`X-Batch: 1`) read their images one at a time; interactive requests fan out in parallel. | A batch request is refused rather than queued when no slot is free, so a two-image batch request that already held one slot was being refused halfway through, which the load test exposed as a 429 storm. **(corrected)** | `docs/LOADTEST.md`; `tests/integration/test_batch_mode.py`. |
    34	| D-026 | 2026-09-03 | The browser batch client limits concurrent requests, not concurrent applications, sizes the pool from `/health`, and backs off with growing delays on 429. The server reserves nothing statically; batch may use every slot unless a person is waiting. | The first design (one reserved slot, item-level concurrency) starved batch on a two-worker host and still produced refusals. Measured in the browser smoke test. **(corrected)** | `app/static/batch.js`; `tests/unit/test_pool.py`. |
    35	| D-027 | 2026-09-03 | Host: Azure Container Apps (2 vCPU / 4 GiB, minReplicas 1), account created on deploy day so the 30-day credit covers the review window; a neutral custom domain fronts it so the submitted URL survives a later move to another host. | Their cloud and the best odds of being reachable from a government network, at no cost for the window that matters; the domain removes the risk of a dead link if hosting changes. | Author decision after the cost correction. |
    36	| D-028 | 2026-09-04 | CI's container job must run its check with stdin attached and fail when the app is not ready; warm-up failures are surfaced in health and readiness. Shell scripts on this machine lose backslashes, so every multi-line edit goes through a script file. **(corrected)** | The first container job passed while running nothing (no `-i` on `docker exec`) and the app never became ready because system libraries were missing; an origin regex had literal backspace bytes from the same shell behavior. Both were caught by the Codex code review and by reading the raw job log. | Review 002; CI runs 33818820324, 33819136086. |
    37	| D-029 | 2026-09-04 | Exact means exact: accents in the OCR read of the warning are noise (Needs review), not stripped before the literal comparison. **(corrected)** | I had stripped accents to keep clean artwork at "exact"; the reviewer was right that a legal status must not paper over an instrument's uncertainty. The evaluation reports the resulting false alarms honestly. | Review 002 item 1.6. |
    38	| D-030 | 2026-09-04 | When the label images of one application state different alcohol contents, that contradiction is a mismatch in itself, regardless of which one agrees with the application. | A front label at 40% and a back label at 45% cannot both be right; preferring the one that matches the application would hide the defect. Percent statements now also need an alcohol-by-volume marker so "5% alcohol flavoring" is not parsed as a statement. | Review 002 item 1.5; integration test wrong_abv. |
    39	| D-031 | 2026-09-04 | A worker slot is held until the OCR thread finishes, even when the client disconnects; batch requests hold one slot for all their images. **(corrected)** | Releasing on cancellation oversubscribed the CPU; per-image admission could refuse a multi-image batch request halfway. Both pinned by unit tests. | Review 002 items 1.1, 1.2. |
    40	| D-032 | 2026-09-04 | The recognizer decodes with its alphabet restricted to printable ASCII, for every line, as a configuration of the OCR engine (`TTB_OCR_ASCII_ALPHABET`, default on). "Exact" keeps its meaning: one transcript, compared literally. **(author's decision, against the reviewer's ranking)** | The multilingual model emitted stray accented letters on clean artwork (2 of 10 labels), turning exact warnings into Needs review. Review 003 ranked "do nothing" first and warned against overriding an accent-capable read with a second opinion; it also proposed a native-resolution re-read, which I tested and which did not converge (a different accent appeared, and a previously exact label lost a letter). Restricting the alphabet is not a second opinion: US labels are English text and the regulation is pure ASCII, so it is the same choice as using an English recognizer, with none of the latency. Genuinely accented print is read as its base letter; comparisons already fold accents and the evidence crop shows the print. | docs/reviews/003-accents.*; experiment in the decision record; evaluation re-run in docs/EVAL.md. |

===== FILE: docs/DEPLOY.md =====
     1	# Deployment runbook: Azure Container Apps
     2	
     3	One container, always on, 2 vCPU / 4 GiB, no state. Commands are Azure CLI; run them from the
     4	repository root after `az login`. Names are examples; keep them lowercase.
     5	
     6	## 0. Prerequisites (once)
     7	
     8	```bash
     9	az extension add --name containerapp --upgrade
    10	az provider register --namespace Microsoft.App
    11	az provider register --namespace Microsoft.OperationalInsights
    12	az provider register --namespace Microsoft.ContainerRegistry
    13	```
    14	
    15	## 1. Resource group and registry
    16	
    17	```bash
    18	LOCATION=eastus
    19	RG=label-check-rg
    20	ACR=labelcheckacr$RANDOM          # must be globally unique, lowercase, 5-50 chars
    21	az group create --name $RG --location $LOCATION
    22	az acr create --name $ACR --resource-group $RG --sku Basic
    23	```
    24	
    25	## 2. Get the image into the registry
    26	
    27	ACR's cloud build (`az acr build`) is disabled on new subscriptions until a support request is
    28	approved. CI builds the same image on every push to `master`, verifies it, and pushes it to the
    29	registry as `label-check:<short sha>` and `label-check:latest`, using three repository secrets:
    30	`ACR_LOGIN_SERVER`, `ACR_USERNAME`, `ACR_PASSWORD` (registry admin credentials; a service principal
    31	with `AcrPush` is the production-grade alternative).
    32	
    33	```bash
    34	az acr update --name $ACR --admin-enabled true
    35	az acr credential show --name $ACR     # username + password -> GitHub secrets
    36	```
    37	
    38	If ACR Tasks are available on the subscription, `az acr build --registry $ACR --image label-check:$SHA --build-arg GIT_SHA=$SHA .` does the same from the repository root.
    39	
    40	## 3. Environment and app
    41	
    42	```bash
    43	ENV=label-check-env
    44	APP=label-check
    45	az containerapp env create --name $ENV --resource-group $RG --location $LOCATION
    46	az containerapp create \
    47	  --name $APP --resource-group $RG --environment $ENV \
    48	  --image $ACR.azurecr.io/label-check:$SHA \
    49	  --registry-server $ACR.azurecr.io --registry-identity system \
    50	  --target-port 8000 --ingress external \
    51	  --cpu 2 --memory 4Gi --min-replicas 1 --max-replicas 1 \
    52	  --env-vars TTB_OCR_WORKERS=2 TTB_TRUST_PROXY=true GIT_SHA=$SHA TTB_OCR_MAX_SIDE=1280
    53	az containerapp show --name $APP --resource-group $RG --query properties.configuration.ingress.fqdn -o tsv
    54	```
    55	
    56	`--registry-identity system` gives the app a managed identity with pull rights on the registry, so
    57	no registry password is stored anywhere.
    58	
    59	## 4. Probes (readiness gates traffic until the models are warm)
    60	
    61	```bash
    62	az containerapp update --name $APP --resource-group $RG --yaml - <<'YAML'
    63	properties:
    64	  template:
    65	    containers:
    66	      - name: label-check
    67	        probes:
    68	          - type: Startup
    69	            httpGet: { path: /api/v1/ready, port: 8000 }
    70	            initialDelaySeconds: 5
    71	            periodSeconds: 5
    72	            failureThreshold: 30
    73	          - type: Readiness
    74	            httpGet: { path: /api/v1/ready, port: 8000 }
    75	            periodSeconds: 10
    76	          - type: Liveness
    77	            httpGet: { path: /api/v1/health, port: 8000 }
    78	            periodSeconds: 30
    79	YAML
    80	```
    81	
    82	(If the YAML update complains about the container name, take it from
    83	`az containerapp show ... --query properties.template.containers[0].name`.)
    84	
    85	## 5. Verify from outside
    86	
    87	```bash
    88	FQDN=$(az containerapp show --name $APP --resource-group $RG --query properties.configuration.ingress.fqdn -o tsv)
    89	curl -s https://$FQDN/api/v1/health | python -m json.tool
    90	python tools/loadtest.py --url https://$FQDN --mode steady --endpoint verify --n 20
    91	python tools/loadtest.py --url https://$FQDN --mode steady --endpoint extract --n 100
    92	python tools/loadtest.py --url https://$FQDN --mode burst --endpoint extract --concurrency 16
    93	```
    94	
    95	Record the numbers in the README "Measured" section and keep `docs/LOADTEST.md`.
    96	
    97	## 6. Custom domain (so the submitted URL survives a later move)
    98	
    99	1. In the DNS provider: `CNAME labelcheck.example -> <FQDN>` and
   100	   `TXT asuid.labelcheck.example -> <verification id>` where the id comes from
   101	   `az containerapp show ... --query properties.customDomainVerificationId -o tsv`.
   102	2. Bind with a free managed certificate:
   103	
   104	```bash
   105	DOMAIN=labelcheck.example
   106	az containerapp hostname add  --name $APP --resource-group $RG --hostname $DOMAIN
   107	az containerapp hostname bind --name $APP --resource-group $RG --hostname $DOMAIN --environment $ENV --validation-method CNAME
   108	```
   109	
   110	Certificate issuance takes a few minutes. Test `https://$DOMAIN/api/v1/health`.
   111	
   112	## 7. Redeploy
   113	
   114	Push to `master`, let CI build, verify and publish `label-check:<short sha>`, then point the app at it:
   115	
   116	```bash
   117	SHA=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)
   118	az containerapp update --name $APP --resource-group $RG --image $ACR.azurecr.io/label-check:$SHA --set-env-vars GIT_SHA=$SHA
   119	```
   120	
   121	The footer and `/api/v1/health` show the SHA, so a reviewer can match the running build to the tag.
   122	
   123	## 8. Freeze, monitor, cost
   124	
   125	- Tag the submitted commit: `git tag -a v1.0-submitted -m "Submitted" && git push --tags`.
   126	  No deploys after that.
   127	- Health monitor: any uptime checker on `https://$DOMAIN/api/v1/health` every 5 minutes.
   128	- Cost: with `min-replicas 1` the app is billed continuously (idle rate when no requests are being
   129	  served). Check the subscription's cost analysis after 24 hours and note the real figure in the
   130	  decision log. Stop billing after the review with `az containerapp update --min-replicas 0` or
   131	  delete the resource group.
   132	
   133	## Notes
   134	
   135	- Multipart parts up to the per-image cap stay in memory (`app/main.py` raises Starlette's spool
   136	  threshold); a larger part is spooled to `/tmp` until the route rejects it. Mount `/tmp` in memory
   137	  where the platform allows it (Container Apps: an ephemeral volume; Docker: `--tmpfs /tmp`).
   138	- The image needs no outbound network at run time. If the environment restricts egress, nothing
   139	  in the app will notice.
   140	- Two vCPUs are the floor for the five-second front+back target; more vCPUs raise batch throughput
   141	  linearly (`TTB_OCR_WORKERS` defaults to the CPU count).

===== FILE: docs/LIMITS.md =====
     1	# Limits, measured behavior, and known weaknesses
     2	
     3	Reviewers will push on this prototype, and they should. This page says what happens when they do.
     4	
     5	## Enforced limits
     6	
     7	| Limit | Value | What happens past it |
     8	|---|---|---|
     9	| Image size | 10 MB per image | HTTP 413, message names the file and the limit |
    10	| Request size | 40 MB, `Content-Length` required | HTTP 413 or 411 before the body is read (six images fit only if together they stay under 40 MB) |
    11	| Image dimensions | 25 megapixels | HTTP 413 "image too large", checked from the header before decoding; large JPEGs decode at reduced size |
    12	| Images per application | 6 | HTTP 422 |
    13	| Image formats | PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, TIFF, BMP by file signature | HTTP 415 with a format-specific hint (PDF, SVG, HEIC named) |
    14	| Concurrent OCR jobs | one per CPU (`TTB_OCR_WORKERS`) | Interactive requests wait up to 8 s for a slot, then 429; batch requests get 429 immediately with `Retry-After` |
    15	| Concurrent requests per client | 4 | HTTP 429 with `Retry-After: 1`, decided before the body is read |
    16	| Concurrent metered requests, all clients | 24 | HTTP 503 with `Retry-After: 2`, decided before the body is read |
    17	| Compare items per call | 100 | HTTP 422 |
    18	| CSV rows / size | 5,000 rows, 2 MB | Rows beyond the limit are ignored with a warning; larger files are refused |
    19	| Batch size in the browser | no hard cap; tested with hundreds of images | Table paginates at 50 rows and re-renders at most a few times per second; result detail (crops, highlights) is rendered only when a row is expanded and cached |
    20	
    21	Health (`/api/v1/health`) never goes through the limiter, so it answers during a flood.
    22	Readiness (`/api/v1/ready`) returns 503 until the OCR engines are warm, so a platform never routes traffic to a cold instance.
    23	
    24	## Measured behavior
    25	
    26	- Accuracy and false-alarm rate by tier: `docs/EVAL.md` (generated by `tools/evaluate.py`).
    27	- Throughput, latency percentiles and the 429 path under load: `docs/LOADTEST.md` (generated by
    28	  `tools/loadtest.py`, blocks name the host they ran against).
    29	- Engine selection and thread scaling: `docs/OCR_EVAL.md` and `docs/BAKEOFF.md`.
    30	- Deployed numbers are in the README under "Measured".
    31	
    32	## Known weaknesses, stated plainly
    33	
    34	1. **Bold type is not assessed.** 27 CFR 16.22 requires "GOVERNMENT WARNING" in bold and the rest
    35	   not in bold. The warning card shows both items as "Not checked" with the rule, so an agent does
    36	   not assume they were verified. A stroke-width heuristic was designed and not built in the time
    37	   available.
    38	2. **Physical type size and characters per inch are not assessed.** They need a known scale.
    39	   Artwork files that carry DPI metadata would allow it; this build does not read DPI.
    40	3. **Photographs are best effort.** The primary input is label artwork as submitted to COLA.
    41	   The engine did well on the degraded test images, and sideways images are recovered by a
    42	   rotation retry, but glare across text or heavy blur will produce "unreadable" with a reason
    43	   rather than a guess.
    44	4. **Arched, curved or heavily stylized brand text** reads less reliably than straight text and
    45	   will land in "Needs review" more often. The evidence crop makes that a two-second decision.
    46	5. **The tool compares; it does not validate designations.** Whether "Kentucky Straight Bourbon
    47	   Whiskey" is a permissible class/type for this product, age statements, name-and-address
    48	   phrasing rules, alcohol-statement wording rules, laboratory tolerances and same-field-of-vision
    49	   placement are out of scope.
    50	6. **Standards of fill are advisory.** The container-size lists changed in January 2025 and stock
    51	   can be grandfathered, so an off-list size is "Needs review", never a failure. The check uses the
    52	   application's stated net contents, not the OCR reading.
    53	7. **English only.** The recognizer handles Latin text with accents; it is not tuned for other
    54	   scripts. Country-of-origin phrasing is compared, not validated against customs rules.
    55	8. **No persistence, by design.** Refreshing the batch page clears the session. The page says so
    56	   and the export is one click.
    57	9. **One container.** Throughput scales with CPUs and with replicas (the service is stateless), but
    58	   this prototype runs one instance. A 1,000-image batch at about two seconds per image on two
    59	   CPUs takes roughly ten to fifteen minutes and streams results the whole time.
    60	10. **Synthetic test corpus.** Ground truth is exact because the labels are rendered, and that also
    61	    means real-world variety is under-represented. Numbers in `docs/EVAL.md` are an upper bound
    62	    for artwork and a rough guide for photographs.
    63	11. **The recognizer's alphabet is restricted to printable ASCII.** The multilingual model occasionally
    64	    emitted stray accented letters on clean English artwork, so decoding is constrained to the characters
    65	    English label text can contain (`TTB_OCR_ASCII_ALPHABET`, on by default). A genuinely accented letter
    66	    on a label, such as a French back label or a brand like Château, is read as its base letter; field
    67	    comparisons already ignore accents and the evidence crop shows the print. Turn the flag off to read
    68	    accented text literally, at the cost of the stray-accent false alarms.
    69	12. **Fuzzy pairing is not attempted.** Images that are not listed in the CSV or named with the
    70	    application reference are shown as unmatched for the agent to assign. Guessing which back label
    71	    belongs to which application would be confidently wrong too often.

===== FILE: docs/REGULATIONS.md =====
     1	# Regulatory basis for the checks
     2	
     3	Verified 2026-09-03 against the eCFR versioner API (point-in-time 2026-08-29). The raw XML for
     4	each section is saved next to this file under `regs/`. Where the app encodes a rule, this is
     5	the source; where a rule cannot be checked from an image, it is listed as out of scope.
     6	
     7	## Health warning statement: 27 CFR Part 16
     8	
     9	**Text (16.21), verbatim:**
    10	
    11	> GOVERNMENT WARNING: (1) According to the Surgeon General, women should not drink alcoholic
    12	> beverages during pregnancy because of the risk of birth defects. (2) Consumption of alcoholic
    13	> beverages impairs your ability to drive a car or operate machinery, and may cause health
    14	> problems.
    15	
    16	16.21 also requires the statement to be "separate and apart from all other information", on the
    17	brand label, a separate front label, or a back or side label.
    18	
    19	**Format (16.22):**
    20	
    21	| Rule | Regulatory language | Checked by the app |
    22	|---|---|---|
    23	| Anchor in capitals and bold | "The first two words ... 'GOVERNMENT WARNING,' shall appear in capital letters and in bold type." | Capitals: yes (from the OCR read). Bold: not in this build; shown as "Not checked" |
    24	| Remainder not bold | "The remainder of the warning statement may not appear in bold type." | Not in this build; shown as "Not checked" |
    25	| Legible, contrasting background | "readily legible under ordinary conditions, and ... on a contrasting background" | Not in this build |
    26	| Not compressed | "shall not be compressed in such a manner that the warning statement is not readily legible" | No (needs physical measurement) |
    27	| Minimum type size | 1 mm for containers of 237 mL or less; 2 mm for more than 237 mL up to 3 L; 3 mm for more than 3 L | Not in this build. Physical mm cannot be measured from an image without a known scale. |
    28	| Max characters per inch | 40 at 1 mm, 25 at 2 mm, 12 at 3 mm | No (needs physical scale) |
    29	| Firmly affixed | labels not removable without water or solvents | No (physical) |
    30	
    31	Amendment history: T.D. ATF-294 (1990), T.D. 372 (1996), T.D. TTB-91 (2011).
    32	
    33	Applicability: Part 16 applies to alcoholic beverages of 0.5 percent or more alcohol by volume
    34	(27 CFR 16.10). When the application states less than 0.5 percent, the app reports the warning as
    35	not required instead of missing.
    36	
    37	## Standards of fill (authorized container sizes)
    38	
    39	The app compares the parsed net contents to these lists and reports "Non-standard container size,
    40	verify" when off-list. Never a failure on its own: the lists change (both were expanded by
    41	T.D. TTB-200 in January 2025) and importers may have grandfathered stock.
    42	
    43	**Distilled spirits, 27 CFR 5.203(a)** (T.D. TTB-200, 90 FR 1876, Jan. 10, 2025), 25 sizes:
    44	3.75 L, 3 L, 2 L, 1.8 L, 1.75 L, 1.5 L, 1.00 L, 945 mL, 900 mL, 750 mL, 720 mL, 710 mL, 700 mL,
    45	570 mL, 500 mL, 475 mL, 375 mL, 355 mL, 350 mL, 331 mL, 250 mL, 200 mL, 187 mL, 100 mL, 50 mL.
    46	Exception 5.203(b): imported spirits in customs custody or bottled before Jan. 1, 1980.
    47	
    48	**Wine, 27 CFR 4.72(a)** (last amended T.D. TTB-200, 90 FR 1875, Jan. 20, 2025), 25 sizes:
    49	3 L, 2.25 L, 1.8 L, 1.5 L, 1 L, 750 mL, 720 mL, 700 mL, 620 mL, 600 mL, 568 mL, 550 mL, 500 mL,
    50	473 mL, 375 mL, 360 mL, 355 mL, 330 mL, 300 mL, 250 mL, 200 mL, 187 mL, 180 mL, 100 mL, 50 mL.
    51	4.72(b): containers of 4 L or larger are allowed in even liters (4, 5, 6 ...).
    52	
    53	**Malt beverages (Part 7):** no standards of fill. The check is skipped for beer.
    54	
    55	## Other mandatory label elements (context only)
    56	
    57	Brand name, class/type designation, alcohol content, net contents, name and address of the
    58	bottler or producer, and country of origin for imports come from 27 CFR Part 5 (spirits), Part 4
    59	(wine) and Part 7 (malt beverages), and TTB's Beverage Alcohol Manuals. The app checks that
    60	these match the application; it does not validate the designation itself, age statements,
    61	name-and-address phrasing, alcohol-content statement wording, or laboratory tolerances
    62	(5.65, 4.36, 7.65). Those are listed in LIMITS.md.
    63	
    64	## Beverage-type differences the app encodes (simplified)
    65	
    66	- Spirits: alcohol content required.
    67	- Wine: alcohol content may be replaced by a "Table Wine" or "Light Wine" designation for
    68	  7 to 14 percent wines. If the application provides a value, it is checked.
    69	- Malt beverages: alcohol content optional under federal rules. If the application provides a
    70	  value, it is checked.
    71	
    72	## Sources
    73	
    74	- eCFR API: `https://www.ecfr.gov/api/versioner/v1/full/2026-08-29/title-27.xml?part=<part>&section=<section>`
    75	- Cornell LII mirror used for cross-reading: `https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/27/<section>`
    76	- TTB labeling resources: `https://www.ttb.gov/regulated-commodities/beverage-alcohol/labeling`

===== FILE: docs/SECURITY.md =====
     1	# Security and data handling
     2	
     3	This is a prototype for a take-home exercise, deployed on a public URL with no authentication,
     4	handling fictional sample data. The design still follows the rules that would apply to a real
     5	deployment where they cost nothing, and it says plainly where it stops.
     6	
     7	## Data handling
     8	
     9	- **Nothing is stored.** Uploaded images are decoded in memory, read by the OCR engine, and
    10	  discarded when the response is sent. There is no database, no upload directory, no cache of
    11	  results. Batch state (files, results, decisions) lives only in the agent's browser tab.
    12	- **Nothing sensitive is logged.** Request logs carry method, path, status and timing. Label text,
    13	  filenames, application values and results never reach a log line.
    14	- **Uploads stay in memory up to the per-image cap.** The web framework spools multipart parts above
    15	  a threshold to a temporary directory while parsing; this app sets that threshold just above the
    16	  10 MB per-image cap, so every accepted image stays in memory. A part larger than that is spooled
    17	  to `/tmp` until the route rejects it, which is why the deployment mounts `/tmp` in memory
    18	  (`--tmpfs /tmp`). Total memory is bounded by the 40 MB request cap times the global cap of 24
    19	  concurrent metered requests, which is enforced before parsing.
    20	- **No outbound calls.** The verification path makes no network connections. OCR models are in
    21	  the repository (hash-pinned in `app/models/MANIFEST.json`); frontend assets are self-hosted. A
    22	  test blocks every socket connection and runs a full verification (`tests/integration/test_no_egress.py`).
    23	
    24	## Controls in this build
    25	
    26	| Control | Where |
    27	|---|---|
    28	| Request body size guard (declared length required, 40 MB cap) and per-image cap (10 MB) enforced while reading | `app/security.py`, `app/routes/api.py` |
    29	| File type by signature, not extension; PDF, SVG, HEIC and unknown types refused with a specific message | `app/pipeline/images.py` |
    30	| Decompression-bomb guard (40 megapixels, checked from the header before decoding) | `app/pipeline/images.py` |
    31	| Corrupt or truncated images fail cleanly with an error envelope, never a stack trace | `app/pipeline/images.py`, `app/security.py` |
    32	| Security headers: Content-Security-Policy (`default-src 'self'`, no inline scripts or styles), `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Permissions-Policy`, cross-origin isolation headers, `Cache-Control: no-store` | `app/security.py` |
    33	| Per-client in-flight cap (4 concurrent requests) with 429 and Retry-After; global cap with 503 | `app/security.py` |
    34	| Capacity limiter with interactive priority: batch never queues, it is refused with 429 when capacity is full or a person is waiting | `app/ocr/pool.py`, `tests/unit/test_pool.py` |
    35	| Capacity is held for as long as an OCR thread actually runs, even if the client disconnects (inference cannot be interrupted, so freeing the slot early would oversubscribe the CPU) | `app/ocr/pool.py`, `tests/unit/test_pool.py` |
    36	| Admission caps enforced in middleware before the request body is parsed: per client (4) and global (24 concurrent metered requests); `Server-Timing` on every response | `app/security.py` |
    37	| Consistent error envelope `{code, message, hint, request_id}`; unhandled errors return a generic message and a request id | `app/security.py` |
    38	| Container runs as a non-root user; only the runtime libraries the slim image needs | `Dockerfile` |
    39	| No inline event handlers or `innerHTML` with user or OCR text; all text rendered via `textContent` | `app/static/*.js` |
    40	| CSV export neutralizes spreadsheet formulas (`= + - @` prefixes) | `app/static/batch.js` |
    41	| Dependencies pinned to exact versions | `requirements.txt` |
    42	| Proxy trust is explicit: client identity from the LAST `X-Forwarded-For` value (the one the trusted ingress appends; the first can be forged) and only when `TTB_TRUST_PROXY=true` | `app/config.py`, `app/security.py` |
    43	
    44	## Deliberately not in this build
    45	
    46	- **Authentication and authorization.** There is no sign-in. A fake login page would demonstrate
    47	  nothing. In production this sits behind the agency's identity provider: Entra ID single sign-on
    48	  with PIV/CAC, enforced at the ingress (Azure Container Apps built-in authentication or an
    49	  application gateway), with roles for agents and supervisors.
    50	- **Audit trail.** Decisions are exported by the agent, not recorded by the server. A production
    51	  version would write an append-only audit record of who checked what and decided what, which is
    52	  also where the retention policy applies.
    53	- **Rate limiting by identity.** Today the caps are per client address. With authentication they
    54	  become per user.
    55	- **Software bill of materials and hash-pinned installs.** Straightforward to add in CI
    56	  (CycloneDX, `pip-compile --generate-hashes`); omitted from the prototype for time.
    57	
    58	## Path to production (summary)
    59	
    60	Azure Government region; private ingress with the identity provider in front; images and results
    61	never leaving the agency boundary (they already never leave the container); a retention policy
    62	for the audit record only; model files versioned and evaluated before each change; the same
    63	container image promoted from test to production; monitoring on the health endpoint and the
    64	`Server-Timing` header every response carries.
    65	
    66	## Reporting a problem
    67	
    68	Open an issue in the repository or contact the author. Do not include real label data in a report.

===== FILE: requirements-ocr.txt =====
     1	# Installed separately with `pip install --no-deps -r requirements-ocr.txt` so that rapidocr's
     2	# declared opencv-python dependency does not pull the desktop OpenCV build next to the headless one.
     3	rapidocr==3.9.2

===== FILE: requirements.txt =====
     1	# Runtime dependencies, pinned.
     2	# rapidocr declares a dependency on the desktop `opencv-python` wheel, which needs display libraries
     3	# (libGL, X11) even in a container. We install rapidocr with --no-deps (see requirements-ocr.txt) and
     4	# list its runtime dependencies here with the headless OpenCV wheel instead.
     5	fastapi==0.141.1
     6	uvicorn[standard]==0.52.4
     7	python-multipart==0.0.32
     8	pydantic==2.13.5
     9	pydantic-settings==2.15.0
    10	jinja2==3.1.6
    11	numpy==2.5.2
    12	opencv-python-headless==5.0.0.93
    13	pillow==12.3.0
    14	rapidfuzz==3.14.6
    15	onnxruntime==1.29.0
    16	# rapidocr runtime dependencies (kept in sync with `pip show rapidocr`)
    17	colorlog==6.10.1
    18	omegaconf==2.3.0
    19	pyclipper==1.4.0
    20	PyYAML==6.0.3
    21	requests==2.32.5
    22	shapely==2.1.2
    23	six==1.17.0
    24	tqdm==4.67.1

===== FILE: tests/browser/README.md =====
     1	# Browser smoke tests
     2	
     3	Headless Chromium checks of the real interface: console errors, CSP violations, failed requests,
     4	the three one-click samples, the demo batch (pairing, details, decisions, export, filters), and
     5	a phone-sized viewport with no horizontal scrolling.
     6	
     7	They need a running server and Playwright with Chromium, so they are not part of CI:
     8	
     9	```bash
    10	pip install playwright && playwright install chromium
    11	uvicorn app.main:app --port 8000 &
    12	LABEL_CHECK_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000 python tests/browser/smoke_single.py
    13	LABEL_CHECK_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000 python tests/browser/smoke_batch.py
    14	```
    15	
    16	Screenshots are written to the current directory (or `LABEL_CHECK_SHOTS`).

===== FILE: tests/browser/batch_scale.py =====
     1	"""Scale run of the batch screen: N applications (2 images each) through the real interface.
     2	
     3	Builds a temporary folder of renamed fixture images plus a CSV, drives the browser through the
     4	batch screen, and reports wall time, per-image latency percentiles as shown by the page, and the
     5	summary tiles. Evidence for the "200 to 300 at once" requirement.
     6	
     7	    LABEL_CHECK_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000 python tests/browser/batch_scale.py --apps 150
     8	"""
     9	
    10	from __future__ import annotations
    11	
    12	import argparse
    13	import csv
    14	import json
    15	import os
    16	import shutil
    17	import sys
    18	import tempfile
    19	import time
    20	import urllib.request
    21	from pathlib import Path
    22	
    23	from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright
    24	
    25	BASE = os.environ.get("LABEL_CHECK_URL", "http://127.0.0.1:8000")
    26	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
    27	FIXTURES = ROOT / "tests" / "fixtures" / "labels"
    28	CSV_FIELDS = [
    29	    "application_id",
    30	    "beverage_type",
    31	    "brand_name",
    32	    "class_type",
    33	    "alcohol_content",
    34	    "net_contents",
    35	    "bottler",
    36	    "country_of_origin",
    37	    "imported",
    38	    "images",
    39	]
    40	
    41	
    42	def build_corpus(n_apps: int, out: Path) -> Path:
    43	    manifest = json.loads((FIXTURES / "manifest.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    44	    apps = manifest["applications"]
    45	    rows = []
    46	    for i in range(n_apps):
    47	        src = apps[i % len(apps)]
    48	        app_id = f"COLA-{i + 1:04d}"
    49	        imgs = {im["side"]: im["file"] for im in src["images"] if im["variant"] == "clean"}
    50	        names = []
    51	        for side in ("front", "back"):
    52	            name = f"{app_id}_{side}.png"
    53	            shutil.copyfile(FIXTURES / imgs[side], out / name)
    54	            names.append(name)
    55	        rows.append(
    56	            {
    57	                "application_id": app_id,
    58	                "beverage_type": src["beverage_type"],
    59	                "brand_name": src["brand"],
    60	                "class_type": src["class_type"],
    61	                "alcohol_content": src["alcohol_content"],
    62	                "net_contents": src["net_contents"],
    63	                "bottler": src["bottler"],
    64	                "country_of_origin": src["origin"].replace("Product of ", ""),
    65	                "imported": "yes" if "USA" not in src["origin"] else "no",
    66	                "images": ";".join(names),
    67	            }
    68	        )
    69	    csv_path = out / "applications.csv"
    70	    with csv_path.open("w", newline="", encoding="utf-8") as f:
    71	        w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=CSV_FIELDS)
    72	        w.writeheader()
    73	        w.writerows(rows)
    74	    return csv_path
    75	
    76	
    77	def wait_ready(seconds: int = 120) -> None:
    78	    for _ in range(seconds):
    79	        try:
    80	            with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{BASE}/api/v1/health", timeout=3) as r:
    81	                if json.load(r).get("ready"):
    82	                    return
    83	        except Exception:
    84	            pass
    85	        time.sleep(1)
    86	    sys.exit("server not ready")
    87	
    88	
    89	def main() -> int:
    90	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    91	    ap.add_argument("--apps", type=int, default=150)
    92	    ap.add_argument("--report", default=str(ROOT / "docs" / "LOADTEST.md"))
    93	    args = ap.parse_args()
    94	    wait_ready()
    95	    tmp = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="label-check-scale-"))
    96	    csv_path = build_corpus(args.apps, tmp)
    97	    images = sorted(p for p in tmp.glob("*.png"))
    98	    print(f"corpus: {args.apps} applications, {len(images)} images in {tmp}")
    99	    problems: list[str] = []
   100	    with sync_playwright() as p:
   101	        browser = p.chromium.launch()
   102	        page = browser.new_context(viewport={"width": 1366, "height": 900}).new_page()
   103	        page.on("pageerror", lambda e: problems.append(f"pageerror: {e}"))
   104	        page.on("console", lambda m: problems.append(f"console.error: {m.text}") if m.type == "error" else None)
   105	        page.on("dialog", lambda d: d.dismiss())
   106	        page.goto(BASE + "/#batch", wait_until="networkidle")
   107	        page.set_input_files("#batch-files", [str(x) for x in images])
   108	        page.set_input_files("#batch-csv", str(csv_path))
   109	        page.wait_for_selector("#batch-csv-summary p")
   110	        print("intake:", page.inner_text("#batch-image-count"), "|", page.inner_text("#batch-csv-summary p"))
   111	        t0 = time.time()
   112	        page.click("#batch-start")
   113	        page.wait_for_selector("#batch-status.is-busy", timeout=60000)
   114	        page.wait_for_selector("#batch-status:not(.is-busy)", timeout=3600000)
   115	        wall = time.time() - t0
   116	        print("status:", page.inner_text("#batch-status"))
   117	        tiles = page.locator(".summary-tile").all_inner_texts()
   118	        print("summary:", " | ".join(t.replace("\n", " ") for t in tiles))
   119	        ready = int(tiles[0].split()[0]) if tiles else -1
   120	        block = "\n".join(
   121	            [
   122	                f"### browser batch: {args.apps} applications x 2 images through the batch screen, host {BASE}",
   123	                f"- wall {wall:.0f} s ({args.apps * 2 / wall:.2f} images/s end to end, including compare calls and rendering)",
   124	                f"- summary tiles: {' | '.join(t.replace(chr(10), ' ') for t in tiles)}",
   125	                f"- {'no browser errors' if not problems else str(len(problems)) + ' browser problems: ' + problems[0][:200]}",
   126	            ]
   127	        )
   128	        print(block)
   129	        with open(args.report, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   130	            f.write(f"{block}\n- run at {time.strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}\n\n")
   131	        browser.close()
   132	    shutil.rmtree(tmp, ignore_errors=True)
   133	    return 0 if not problems and ready == args.apps else 1
   134	
   135	
   136	if __name__ == "__main__":
   137	    sys.exit(main())

===== FILE: tests/browser/smoke_batch.py =====
     1	"""Headless browser smoke test of the batch screen.
     2	
     3	Loads the five-application demo batch, waits for completion, checks the summary and rows, opens a
     4	detail panel (crops, highlights), records a decision, exports the CSV, and applies a filter.
     5	Needs a running server and Playwright with Chromium.
     6	
     7	    LABEL_CHECK_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000 python tests/browser/smoke_batch.py
     8	"""
     9	
    10	from __future__ import annotations
    11	
    12	import json
    13	import os
    14	import sys
    15	import time
    16	import urllib.request
    17	
    18	from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright
    19	
    20	BASE = os.environ.get("LABEL_CHECK_URL", "http://127.0.0.1:8000")
    21	OUT = os.environ.get("LABEL_CHECK_SHOTS", ".")
    22	
    23	
    24	def wait_ready(seconds: int = 120) -> None:
    25	    for _ in range(seconds):
    26	        try:
    27	            with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{BASE}/api/v1/health", timeout=3) as r:
    28	                if json.load(r).get("ready"):
    29	                    return
    30	        except Exception:
    31	            pass
    32	        time.sleep(1)
    33	    sys.exit("server not ready")
    34	
    35	
    36	def main() -> int:
    37	    wait_ready()
    38	    problems: list[str] = []
    39	    with sync_playwright() as p:
    40	        browser = p.chromium.launch()
    41	        ctx = browser.new_context(viewport={"width": 1366, "height": 900}, accept_downloads=True)
    42	        page = ctx.new_page()
    43	        page.on(
    44	            "console",
    45	            lambda m: problems.append(f"console.{m.type}: {m.text}") if m.type in ("error", "warning") else None,
    46	        )
    47	        page.on("pageerror", lambda e: problems.append(f"pageerror: {e}"))
    48	        page.on("requestfailed", lambda r: problems.append(f"requestfailed: {r.url} {r.failure}"))
    49	        page.on("response", lambda r: problems.append(f"http {r.status}: {r.url}") if r.status >= 400 else None)
    50	        page.on("dialog", lambda d: d.dismiss())
    51	        page.goto(BASE + "/#batch", wait_until="networkidle")
    52	        page.wait_for_selector("#view-batch:not([hidden])")
    53	        page.click("#batch-demo")
    54	        page.wait_for_selector("#batch-status.is-busy", timeout=30000)
    55	        page.wait_for_selector("#batch-status:not(.is-busy)", timeout=300000)
    56	        print("status:", page.inner_text("#batch-status"))
    57	        print("progress:", page.inner_text("#batch-progress .progress__text"))
    58	        tiles = page.locator(".summary-tile").all_inner_texts()
    59	        print("summary:", " | ".join(t.replace("\n", " ") for t in tiles))
    60	        rows = page.locator(".batch-table > tbody > tr:not(.detail-row)")
    61	        print("rows:", rows.count())
    62	        for i in range(rows.count()):
    63	            cells = rows.nth(i).locator("td").all_inner_texts()
    64	            print("   ", " | ".join(c.replace("\n", " / ")[:70] for c in cells[:4]))
    65	        page.screenshot(path=f"{OUT}/ui_batch.png", full_page=True)
    66	
    67	        page.locator(".batch-table button:has-text('Details')").first.click()
    68	        page.wait_for_selector(".detail-panel .checklist", timeout=30000)
    69	        print(
    70	            "detail crops:",
    71	            page.locator(".detail-panel img.crop").count(),
    72	            "polygons:",
    73	            page.locator(".detail-panel .overlay polygon").count(),
    74	        )
    75	        page.locator(".batch-table .decision-btns button:has-text('Approve')").first.click()
    76	        with page.expect_download() as dl:
    77	            page.click("#batch-export")
    78	        with open(dl.value.path(), encoding="utf-8-sig") as f:
    79	            text = f.read()
    80	        lines = text.splitlines()
    81	        print("export lines:", len(lines) - 1, "| header:", lines[0][:80])
    82	        print("export row 1:", lines[1][:160])
    83	        page.click("[data-filter=attention]")
    84	        print("attention rows:", page.locator(".batch-table > tbody > tr:not(.detail-row)").count())
    85	        browser.close()
    86	
    87	    print("\nPROBLEMS:" if problems else "\nno problems detected")
    88	    for x in problems[:30]:
    89	        print(" -", x[:300])
    90	    return 1 if problems else 0
    91	
    92	
    93	if __name__ == "__main__":
    94	    sys.exit(main())

===== FILE: tests/browser/smoke_single.py =====
     1	"""Headless browser smoke test of the single-application screen.
     2	
     3	Checks: no console errors, no CSP violations, no failed requests; the three one-click samples reach
     4	their expected verdicts with crops and highlights; row selection highlights evidence; a phone-sized
     5	viewport does not scroll horizontally. Needs a running server and Playwright with Chromium.
     6	
     7	    LABEL_CHECK_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000 python tests/browser/smoke_single.py
     8	"""
     9	
    10	from __future__ import annotations
    11	
    12	import json
    13	import os
    14	import sys
    15	import time
    16	import urllib.request
    17	
    18	from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright
    19	
    20	BASE = os.environ.get("LABEL_CHECK_URL", "http://127.0.0.1:8000")
    21	OUT = os.environ.get("LABEL_CHECK_SHOTS", ".")
    22	EXPECTED = {"clean": "Ready for your approval", "photo": None, "problem": "Issues found"}
    23	
    24	
    25	def wait_ready(seconds: int = 120) -> None:
    26	    for _ in range(seconds):
    27	        try:
    28	            with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{BASE}/api/v1/health", timeout=3) as r:
    29	                if json.load(r).get("ready"):
    30	                    return
    31	        except Exception:
    32	            pass
    33	        time.sleep(1)
    34	    sys.exit("server not ready")
    35	
    36	
    37	def main() -> int:
    38	    wait_ready()
    39	    problems: list[str] = []
    40	    with sync_playwright() as p:
    41	        browser = p.chromium.launch()
    42	        ctx = browser.new_context(viewport={"width": 1366, "height": 900})
    43	        page = ctx.new_page()
    44	        page.on(
    45	            "console",
    46	            lambda m: problems.append(f"console.{m.type}: {m.text}") if m.type in ("error", "warning") else None,
    47	        )
    48	        page.on("pageerror", lambda e: problems.append(f"pageerror: {e}"))
    49	        page.on("requestfailed", lambda r: problems.append(f"requestfailed: {r.url} {r.failure}"))
    50	        page.on("response", lambda r: problems.append(f"http {r.status}: {r.url}") if r.status >= 400 else None)
    51	        page.on("dialog", lambda d: d.dismiss())
    52	        page.goto(BASE + "/", wait_until="networkidle")
    53	        print("title:", page.title())
    54	        page.screenshot(path=f"{OUT}/ui_home.png", full_page=True)
    55	
    56	        for sample, expect in EXPECTED.items():
    57	            page.click(f"[data-sample={sample}]")
    58	            page.wait_for_selector("#status.is-busy", timeout=15000)
    59	            page.wait_for_selector("#status:not(.is-busy)", timeout=60000)
    60	            page.wait_for_selector("#results:not([hidden]) .verdict", timeout=60000)
    61	            heading = page.inner_text(".verdict .usa-alert__heading")
    62	            timing = page.inner_text(".verdict .timing").strip()[:60]
    63	            rows = page.locator(".checklist tbody tr").count()
    64	            crops = page.locator(".checklist img.crop").count()
    65	            polys = page.locator(".overlay polygon").count()
    66	            print(f"[{sample}] {heading!r} | {timing} | rows={rows} crops={crops} polygons={polys}")
    67	            if expect and heading != expect:
    68	                problems.append(f"sample {sample}: expected {expect!r}, got {heading!r}")
    69	            page.screenshot(path=f"{OUT}/ui_{sample}.png", full_page=True)
    70	            page.locator(".checklist tbody tr").first.click()
    71	            print("   active polygons after row click:", page.locator(".overlay polygon.is-active").count())
    72	
    73	        mobile = browser.new_context(
    74	            viewport={"width": 390, "height": 844}, device_scale_factor=2, bypass_csp=True
    75	        ).new_page()
    76	        mobile.goto(BASE + "/", wait_until="networkidle")
    77	        mobile.click("[data-sample=clean]")
    78	        mobile.wait_for_selector("#results:not([hidden]) .verdict", timeout=60000)
    79	        mobile.screenshot(path=f"{OUT}/ui_mobile.png", full_page=True)
    80	        if mobile.evaluate("document.documentElement.scrollWidth > document.documentElement.clientWidth"):
    81	            problems.append("mobile: page scrolls horizontally")
    82	        browser.close()
    83	
    84	    print("\nPROBLEMS:" if problems else "\nno problems detected")
    85	    for x in problems[:40]:
    86	        print(" -", x[:300])
    87	    return 1 if problems else 0
    88	
    89	
    90	if __name__ == "__main__":
    91	    sys.exit(main())

===== FILE: tests/integration/test_verify_api.py =====
     1	"""End-to-end checks through the API with the real OCR engine on committed fixtures."""
     2	
     3	from __future__ import annotations
     4	
     5	import pytest
     6	
     7	from tests.integration.conftest import app_json, image_files
     8	
     9	pytestmark = pytest.mark.integration
    10	
    11	
    12	def _app(manifest, app_id):
    13	    return next(a for a in manifest["applications"] if a["id"] == app_id)
    14	
    15	
    16	def _check(resp, check_id):
    17	    return next(c for c in resp["checks"] if c["id"] == check_id)
    18	
    19	
    20	def test_clean_front_and_back_is_ready_for_approval(client, manifest):
    21	    app = _app(manifest, "APP-001")  # Old Tom Distillery, the brief's sample
    22	    r = client.post(
    23	        "/api/v1/verify",
    24	        data={"application": app_json(app)},
    25	        files=image_files("APP-001_front_clean.png", "APP-001_back_clean.png"),
    26	    )
    27	    assert r.status_code == 200, r.text
    28	    body = r.json()
    29	    assert body["verdict"] == "ready_for_approval", body["summary"]
    30	    assert body["warning"]["present"] and body["warning"]["exact"]
    31	    for cid in ("brand_name", "class_type", "alcohol_content", "net_contents", "bottler", "country_of_origin"):
    32	        assert _check(body, cid)["status"] == "match", (cid, _check(body, cid))
    33	    assert all(c["evidence"] for c in body["checks"] if c["status"] == "match")
    34	    assert body["timing"]["total_ms"] < 15000  # generous for CI; the deployed gate is measured separately
    35	
    36	
    37	def test_wrong_abv_on_label_is_an_issue(client, manifest):
    38	    app = _app(manifest, "APP-001")
    39	    r = client.post(
    40	        "/api/v1/verify",
    41	        data={"application": app_json(app)},
    42	        files=image_files("APP-001_front_wrong_abv.png", "APP-001_back_clean.png"),
    43	    )
    44	    body = r.json()
    45	    assert body["verdict"] == "issues_found"
    46	    assert _check(body, "alcohol_content")["status"] == "mismatch"
    47	
    48	
    49	def test_title_case_warning_anchor_needs_review(client, manifest):
    50	    app = _app(manifest, "APP-002")
    51	    r = client.post(
    52	        "/api/v1/verify",
    53	        data={"application": app_json(app)},
    54	        files=image_files("APP-002_front_clean.png", "APP-002_back_titlecase.png"),
    55	    )
    56	    body = r.json()
    57	    assert body["warning"]["present"]
    58	    assert body["warning"]["anchor_caps"] == "needs_review"
    59	    assert body["verdict"] in ("needs_review", "issues_found")
    60	
    61	
    62	def test_altered_warning_wording_is_an_issue(client, manifest):
    63	    app = _app(manifest, "APP-004")
    64	    r = client.post(
    65	        "/api/v1/verify",
    66	        data={"application": app_json(app)},
    67	        files=image_files("APP-004_front_clean.png", "APP-004_back_altered.png"),
    68	    )
    69	    body = r.json()
    70	    assert body["warning"]["present"] and not body["warning"]["exact"]
    71	    assert "-may" in (body["warning"]["diff"] or "")
    72	    assert body["verdict"] == "issues_found"
    73	
    74	
    75	def test_missing_warning_is_an_issue(client, manifest):
    76	    app = _app(manifest, "APP-006")
    77	    r = client.post(
    78	        "/api/v1/verify",
    79	        data={"application": app_json(app)},
    80	        files=image_files("APP-006_front_clean.png", "APP-006_back_missing.png"),
    81	    )
    82	    body = r.json()
    83	    assert not body["warning"]["present"]
    84	    assert body["verdict"] == "issues_found"
    85	
    86	
    87	def test_case_only_brand_difference_is_a_match_with_note(client, manifest):
    88	    """Dave's case: application says Stone's Throw, label says STONE'S THROW."""
    89	    app = _app(manifest, "APP-002")
    90	    r = client.post(
    91	        "/api/v1/verify",
    92	        data={"application": app_json(app, brand_name="Stone's Throw")},
    93	        files=image_files("APP-002_front_clean.png", "APP-002_back_clean.png"),
    94	    )
    95	    body = r.json()
    96	    brand = _check(body, "brand_name")
    97	    assert brand["status"] == "match" and "case" in brand["note"]
    98	
    99	
   100	def test_sideways_image_is_recovered_by_rotation_retry(client, manifest):
   101	    app = _app(manifest, "APP-004")
   102	    r = client.post(
   103	        "/api/v1/verify",
   104	        data={"application": app_json(app)},
   105	        files=image_files("APP-004_front_clean.png", "APP-004_back_rotate90.png"),
   106	    )
   107	    body = r.json()
   108	    back = body["images"][1]
   109	    assert back["rotated_degrees"] in (90, 270)
   110	    assert body["warning"]["present"]
   111	
   112	
   113	def test_front_only_reports_warning_missing_with_guidance(client, manifest):
   114	    app = _app(manifest, "APP-001")
   115	    r = client.post("/api/v1/verify", data={"application": app_json(app)}, files=image_files("APP-001_front_clean.png"))
   116	    body = r.json()
